This is the *inbox* bucket of the [[id:D4219199-7B17-4A89-B4BC-6019738642DA][product backlog]]. Every capture created
via =compass capture --note "..."= lands here. Inbox captures have not yet
been triaged — they are ideas or observations waiting to be assessed.

At triage, move each capture to one of:
- =next/= — ready to pull into an upcoming sprint.
- =deferred/= — real but not soon; long-term horizon.
- =discarded/= — explicitly decided against.

Or promote directly to a story via =compass capture promote <slug>=.

#+end_note

| [[id:41FB0924-C9F6-4884-BA8C-BEAC71A6B6AD][--reset-goldens deletes the golden dataset and nothing recreates it]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | =ore= =testing= =codegen=                                                     | Twelve golden round-trip suites under projects/ores.ore/core/tests/ print "Golden file missing (run with --reset-goldens to bootstrap)" when a golden is absent. The flag is real, but it lives on projects/ores.codegen/scripts/xsdcpp_generate_ore.sh, not on the test binary, and all it does is delete assets/test_data/golden_dataset/Products. No suite writes a golden back: each one calls REQUIRE(std::filesystem::exists(gpath)) and fails. So following the message destroys the dataset it points at, and the message never names the command it means. Only xml_example_coverage_tests.cpp writes missing goldens, and only when ORES_BOOTSTRAP_GOLDENS is set. Pick one: give the flag a bootstrap mode the twelve suites honour, or change the message to name the command that recreates the dataset.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| [[id:F1523C7D-5DA9-42C1-8928-45C1642D4B64][87 non-nullable columns still generate an indeterminate member]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | =codegen= =valgrind= =models= =tech_debt=                                     | Only :default_value: reaches a generated struct's member initializer, and codegen synthesises one for bool and int alone. 87 non-nullable columns across the catalogue declare neither, so a default-constructed object of the type they belong to holds indeterminate members that are only found when a write path reads them -- which is how the 2026-09-29 nightly found 214 reads in ores.analytics.core.tests and 99 in ores.ore.core.tests. The valgrind hotfix closed the instances it reported and gated the nullable shape; the class remains.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

have no gate. regenerate_cmake_component_files.py covers the file lists only, and the workspace clean-up had to render the three parts by hand. | [[id:110DBD58-BD49-41A0-90F4-701116517965][A composite's parts were never enumerated by codegen discove…]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |                                                                               | A composite's parts were never enumerated by codegen discovery. manifest.discover_models globs the composite's modeling_dir one level deep, so ores.<comp>/{api,core,service,client,adapter}/modeling/component_overview.org was never rendered. Their export.hpp, tests/main.cpp and umbrella headers therefore carry every template change since the part was last generated: at ores.iam/api those files were last written 2026-06-05, while the templates changed 2026-08-02. A full regeneration flushes about 250 files -- the generated-file banner (114), licence rewrapping, the OpenSSL test-main cleanup (22), the database lifecycle listener (20), and test_timeout_listener removal (14). Discovery now reaches the parts; the flush, and each component's reconciliation, are the separate work.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |

| [[id:3495D0A3-7B33-48FD-968B-3E5504009A0C][A database recreate fails while the fleet's pools are still closing]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | =tooling= =compass= =database= =reliability=                                  | compass db recreate refuses to drop a database that has connections to it. Stopping the fleet does not close its pooled connections at once, so a recreate started soon after a stop aborts with a few active connections even though --kill was passed. Waiting until the database has no other connections makes it succeed; on this machine that took between ten and forty seconds after the stop.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |

| [[id:79E4788A-BC24-47B0-B900-E833D3A98A9F][A freshly recreated database has no materialised calendar da…]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |                                                                               | A freshly recreated database has no materialised calendar dates, so every tenor resolution refuses with calendar_not_materialised until somebody rebuilds a calendar. Verified 2026-10-18: after compass db recreate and the ACME provisioning script, ores_refdata_calendar_dates_tbl was empty across all 122 calendars, and every preview and resolve call against any calendar refused. One rebuild fixed it: POST /api/refdata/calendars/Argentina/rebuild with endYear 2027 wrote 1461 dates spanning 2024 to 2027 and the same call then resolved. The journey's test step and the new resolve screen both therefore refuse on a brand new environment, and the refusal is correct but reads as a broken screen. Provisioning should materialise at least the calendars the seed conventions roll on, or the rebuild should run as a provisioning step, or the screens should offer the rebuild where they report the refusal.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| [[id:D42BD56B-584A-4CEF-8257-64AE14850DD9][A generated eventing integration test leaves the parent row it seeds active]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | =codegen= =tests=                                                             | The codegen template that writes the eventing integration tests seeds a soft-FK parent row and closes only the row under test. Because a generated entity's code restarts its sequence every run, the parent left active makes a second run against the same database fail on the duplicate code. component's own enum types, so trade_types add, set and put-many abort at the client. | [[id:0EBEBF7B-EF41-4088-9CCF-CEB054D4635E][A generated shell command with no arguments wraps an empty try block]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | =codegen= =shell=                                                             | The shell operation command generator emits a try/catch around the block that populates a request from the parsed arguments. A request model with no fields produces no statements in that block, so the generated command contains 'try { } catch (const std::exception& e) { ... }' with nothing between the braces. It is harmless but it is noise in every no-argument command, and the first one is authorization_operations_commands::process_get_my_roles, generated from iam.v1.roles.mine. The candidate is for the command template to skip the block when the request declares no fields. The generator lives in projects/ores.codegen/library/templates/, so the fix is a template change plus a regeneration, not a hand edit of the generated file.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| [[id:C8681960-D245-4FD2-9D2D-5CC695E8FA74][A junction's timestamp column is not mapped to sqlgen::Timestamp]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | =codegen= =generator-defect= =junction= =tech_debt=                           | The junction C++ entity template emits a raw std::chrono::time_point for a junction's own timestamp column, which sqlgen cannot serialise, so ores.assets.image_tag fails to compile in image_tag_repository.cpp. The same column type on a domain entity is correctly emitted as std::optional<sqlgen::Timestamp<...>> (compare ores.compute host last_rpc_time with ores.assets image_tag assigned_at). The entity path sets is_optional_timestamp and is_required_timestamp in core.py around line 2418; the junction column-decoration block near line 4595 sets is_int, is_uuid, is_optional_uuid and is_date but never the timestamp flags. Adding them there did NOT change the generated output, so the C++ context for a junction is built on another path that still needs finding. Per G05 the fix belongs in the codegen source with a regression test in the codegen suite.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

Fixing it needs a lexical double leaf in the ORE binding, which is work on the reapply script and the binding's double writer, not an entity unit.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

| [[id:2D3DAEF6-0A2A-44B3-8462-458FED477EB6][A lower-case org id passes lint and stops the site build]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | =docs= =site= =lint=                                                          | compass lint reports every org id link as resolving, and org export refuses the same link. An id spelled in lower case is accepted by the lint and unresolvable by the exporter, which aborts the whole site build on the page that carries it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| [[id:5789D6D0-1967-4064-B349-F97B896A1C67][A market-wide arrival regime, so the desk can panic together]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | =synthetic= =arrival= =hawkes= =regime= =scenario= =risk=                     | The arrival processes built so far model one stream, so each config row clusters on its own events and nothing makes FX, IR and credit burst at the same time. A fast market or a panic is exactly that: every series' intensity rises together for a while. The approach note already lists Regime-switching as a tick mode (two-state active/quiet with a state-transition matrix, lambda_active, lambda_quiet) but no code implements it. Options, cheapest first: (1) one shared market-state machine per run, calm/active, scaling every feed's baseline by a per-asset sensitivity; (2) a common-factor intensity added to every baseline, a shared shock stream that decays; (3) full multivariate Hawkes with a kernel matrix, faithful but O(N^2) and needing multivariate thinning. Option 1 matches the note's vocabulary and gives observable panic windows for scenario and QA runs; option 3 is the only one where one series' events excite another's. All three need the per-row arrival parameters first, so they sit on top of the current task rather than inside it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| [[id:8D5AA7C2-0D67-4348-9562-D128D3B7ADFB][A message may name a type declared later in its model]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | =codegen= =modeling= =lint=                                                   | The generators emit a model's structs in the order the file states them, so a response declared above the type it carries produces a header that does not compile. Every documentation gate passes; only the compiler catches it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |

The globals come from the define block of vite.config.ts and nothing assigns them at runtime.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |

its callback is a use-after-free of the subscription's own storage, so keeping a callback's captures alive is not enough. | [[id:50148376-3D8B-44A9-A851-2D38CE997B9B][A notify trigger collides when a natural key is called key]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | =codegen= =generator-defect= =notify-trigger= =tech_debt=                     | The notify-trigger template declares changed_key jsonb and derives a changed_<natural key column> variable for the entity's key. An entity whose natural key column is called key therefore declares changed_key twice, once text and once jsonb, and create function fails with duplicate declaration. Found on ores.assets.image. Fix belongs in the template by namespacing the payload variable, but that rewrites the 210 generated notify triggers tree-wide plus iam, so the assets story renamed its column to code and filed this instead.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| [[id:E6BB7228-4C00-41A6-9CD7-6D0CC4B515F9][A primary-key column never takes its default]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |                                                                               | The domain template emits a primary-key column as 'type name;' with no initialiser, while every other column carries the ':default_value:' guard. So the one column where an unset value is most likely, and most damaging, is the one that cannot default: a missed assignment reads an indeterminate int instead of failing a constraint. Found while giving a new key column a default of zero.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| [[id:820AC660-C3FC-4A45-998D-A65818FA4BB8][A profile image over the payload cap cannot reach the validator]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |                                                                               | The deployment's NATS takes the default 1 MB max_payload, and the upload body carries the image base64 so it grows by a third: an image over roughly three quarters of a megabyte fails in the transport with MAX_PAYLOAD_EXCEEDED and the caller gets a 503, so the validator's own image_too_large answer is unreachable while the picker and the validator both state 2 MB. Direction (product owner, 2026-10-05): an upload larger than 1 MB travels over HTTP storage, not the message fabric.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |

| [[id:BA9B89D7-D96D-49A8-A821-215103DDF768][A record's measured numbers go stale when trunk moves under the branch]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | =agile= =process= =verification=                                              | Two independent verification rounds failed the ores.service record for the same reason: counts measured on a tree that trunk then moved out from under. The branch was rebased four times while it waited, and each advance changed a consumer count, a gate's component count or a suite's size. Nothing in the record said which tree a number was measured on, so the drift was silent until a verifier re-ran the commands.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |

| [[id:F98C1C83-9847-44C1-ACD0-5E7DE2534AFF][A restored environment arrives in bootstrap mode with no human admin]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | =provisioning= =db= =snag=                                                    | After a database recreate the database came back in bootstrap mode with no human administrator, so a soak run could not log in until the documented bootstrap create-initial-admin step was performed mid-run. A freshly restored environment that nobody can authenticate against is a provisioning gap, and the step is discoverable only if you already know it exists. Seed the initial admin as part of the restore, or have the restore report that it is in bootstrap mode.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |

| [[id:252C3441-D862-4862-9A7A-EE548AC5A799][A run whose dependency is dead reports a timeout, not the cause]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |                                                                               | The party stage waits on the bundle run it published for 1500s and then fails with 'timed out after 1500s; 1/3 steps completed; last step: marketdata.fx_driver_rates (in_progress)', while the real reason sits in another process's log: the marketdata service had exited on a schema error before the run started. Nothing ties a workflow step to the liveness of the service that owns its command subject.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| [[id:63119568-8390-48FE-9595-1440BA98F562][A running fleet reports as missing and start times out]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |                                                                               | In a sandboxed session compass services status and compass bearings classify every unit as missing even while the whole fleet is up: systemctl --user cannot reach the user manager and _unit_active_state returns missing rather than reporting the query failure. That reads as 'nothing is running' when the opposite is true. A follow-up compass services start --use-busctl then waits the full 301s and reports '23/23 still not ready', because _wait_for_logs looks for a Service ready. line appended after start_pos and the already-running processes never log another one; the logs still hold only the previous run's readiness line. The timeout also suggests compass services stop, inviting the operator to kill a healthy fleet. Confirmed on this worktree: status said missing=24 while ports 20205 and 20202 were listening and the services were logging normally. Fix is to distinguish 'query failed' from 'unit missing', and to treat an already-active unit as ready instead of waiting for a fresh log line.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |

| [[id:486638A2-A667-4E41-928A-037EA9A1346D][A stale publish tree runs code the source no longer has]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | =compass= =build= =services= =http= =defect=                                  | ores.http.server failed to start asking for a JWT secret. | [[id:AD2AB0B8-B506-4497-8A33-F847C58D429A][A step command published while its service is stopped is lost]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | =workflow= =engine= =correctness= =bug= =reliability=                         | The engine publishes a step command fire-and-forget to the subject its definition names. If the service that serves that subject is not subscribed at that instant, the command is gone: nothing retries it, nothing reports it, and the run waits for ever on a step that will never answer. Recovery re-dispatches an in-progress step, but only when the workflow service restarts, so the run needs an operator to notice and restart the engine. A step's command must be held until the far end acknowledges it, or a step whose handler has not answered within its budget must be re-dispatched.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| [[id:404C8F41-8EB7-422F-8347-183173F49E3D][A step's wait outlives the engine's deadline]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | =workflow= =iam= =provisioning= =defect= =observability=                      | A provisioning step waits 7200 seconds for a command it publishes, while the workflow engine expires a step at 1200. A command that is never received is therefore reported as twenty minutes of silence rather than a named failure, which is how a subject mismatch between a stale service and the engine stayed invisible.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| [[id:FA7D1F14-B357-4BEA-8AF0-B9D7C16E3E4D][A subject rename leaves durable JetStream consumers unable to attach]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |                                                                               | Six of the twenty-five services in an environment that has been running across the subject rename do not start, and every one of them dies at the same call: the workflow service and all five compute wrappers fail with js_QueueSubscribe failed: Error. The cause is not the stream. The durable consumers that live in the JetStream file store were created before the rename and still carry the old filter subjects, and JetStream refuses to attach a durable whose filter differs from the one the code now asks for. Measured on eager_maxwell: the workflow-engine-step-completed consumer had Filter Subject workflow.v1.events.step-completed while the service subscribes to workflow.v1.ops.step_completed, and compute_wrapper_shared_x64-linux had compute.v1.work.assignments.*.x64-linux against compute.v1.ops.work_assignment.*.x64-linux. Commit 964d526a39 reconciles a stream's subject filter, and it worked -- the streams carry the new subjects -- but nothing reconciles a consumer's, so the store keeps a filter the code can no longer match. Deleting the four stale consumers and restarting brings the services up, which is a hand fix every environment must repeat. The stream reconcile is the shape the fix should take: on attach, compare the stored filter with the one being asked for and delete and recreate the consumer when they differ, since a consumer carries no state worth keeping across a rename (all four had zero ack-pending and zero unprocessed). |

| [[id:25870CC8-06CF-4921-8804-878F2CF62401][A version conflict carries no message]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |                                                                               | The store answers a stale claim with outcome conflict and code version_conflict, and with an empty message. | [[id:256182B2-935B-4EC6-ABB6-7E0DC5C8F553][An account can hold a TOTP secret and nothing can enrol, prove or revoke it]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | =iam= =security= =totp= =credentials= =login=                                 | iam.account_credential carries totp_secret, nullable and :no_wire:, and no code path in the tree reads or writes it: there is no enrolment, no code verification and no way to turn a second factor off. The column is a placeholder. The credentials split has just put the secret on the entity that owns it and given it a version stream of its own, so the seam is in the right place; what is missing is the operations, the login contract change, and a decision about how a secret that cannot be hashed is held at rest.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| [[id:7BF90096-CF2D-476E-9E0E-D403BC24F3D5][An archetype placed in a facet that every component renders …]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |                                                                               | An archetype placed in a facet that every component renders makes the local check gate verify every component at every address, because check_component_drift.py --changed selects the components the branch touched. Adding the umbrella header to ores.cpp.component therefore drags 94 components into the gate at once, and any component whose generated files main hand-edited then fails it -- geo, ores.history/api and ores.storage/service all do. A change of this shape should land as the mechanism alone: the discovery fix, the guards and the template, touching no component file, so the changed set stays empty and the gate passes. Each component then regenerates, and reconciles its hand-edited files, inside its own clean-up task. The bulk flush of about 250 presence files is real work, but it belongs to those tasks, not to the enabling change.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| [[id:B9B8E238-F32B-40AC-88F2-6C210879B67A][An asset class is two lists, not one]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | =asset-class= =taxonomy= =refdata= =marketdata=                               | The product asset class taxonomy is authoritative, runtime-managed and FK-validated, and it is the list a market series takes its class from. The market data namespace is a different and much looser axis: the key grammar an oresmd_uri authority names, accreted from ORE's own key-prefix conventions rather than from any product taxonomy. The two share five spellings and diverge on the rest, and nothing in the repository declares the mapping between them.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

| [[id:12666A97-69C2-4874-ABE0-E8C497C9EAE1][Authentication is optional at runtime on both transports]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | =security= =auth= =http= =nats= =defect=                                      | The HTTP server skips its whole auth middleware block when no authenticator is configured, and warns rather than refusing; the NATS request context comes back unauthenticated when no verifier is configured; and has_permission returns true for an empty permission list to keep pre-RBAC tokens working. | [[id:3E4BE9A0-C14F-400D-8AA7-A1B0D2AB183F][Build a rate blending service. Several simulated providers (Bloomberg, Refinitiv, ICE, the exchanges) publish their own spot for a pair such as EUR/USD. A desk prices off one rate, so a rate blending service should read the providers' spots, blend them by a configured rule (a weighted mid, the best bid and offer, a median of the fresh quotes, with staleness and outlier rejection), and publish the blended EUR/USD as its owner. Until it exists, the conflict guard on synthetic collections lets one bound system collection run, so one provider is bound, chosen arbitrarily, as the authoritative source and the others run sandboxed. | [[id:BBBD07EF-09E0-4FF6-A282-28534429264C][check_component_drift.py corrupts a branch that is behind main]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |                                                                               | Running projects/ores.codegen/scripts/check_component_drift.py with the component flag on a branch whose codegen templates are older than main's regenerates the whole component in place from the stale templates. It rewrote 67 tracked files and materialized 44 untracked ones under ores.trading, reverting newer generated shapes (the trade sub-struct split) and dropping hand-written protocol types (export_portfolio_request). A build running concurrently then failed on the clobbered headers. The script is only safe for components in its KNOWN_DRIFT_FREE registry, which requires zero drift; any other component needs the single-entity compass codegen entity generate command instead. Recovered by stashing the fallout; a matching stash entry dated 2026-09-11 shows this has bitten before.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |

| [[id:E8349FE4-DD8D-41AA-8194-7BB4AC7BC488][Codegen: auto-derive most Qt drawer fields instead of requir…]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |                                                                               | Codegen: auto-derive most Qt drawer fields instead of requiring them spelled out per entity. domain_include/domain_class/protocol_include are pure functions of component and entity_singular. get/save/delete/history request/response/message_type class names are pure functions of entity_plural/entity_singular plus the standard dq::messaging naming convention already used identically by every entity's generated protocol header. collection_name/item_var are typically just entity_plural/entity_singular themselves. Only key_field, has_uuid_primary_key, settings_group, window_title, icon, and the has_ Qt knobs are genuinely entity-specific and need spelling out. Discovered while manually authoring near-identical Qt drawers for catalog, change_reason, change_reason_category, data_domain, and dataset_bundle -- the boilerplate was copy-pasted with only entity-name substitutions each time, exactly the drift-prone pattern profiles exist to eliminate.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| [[id:BB0C3B46-12F2-41CA-9DC4-809536407A33][Codegen: has_parent_id without its two column properties fails silently]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | =codegen= =variability= =guard=                                               | Turning has_parent_id on emits a recursive-CTE hierarchy function that names the entity's parent_id_column and hierarchy_name_field. If the model sets the feature without those two properties, the function is generated with empty column names and nothing fails. Eleven entities sat in exactly that state, hidden only by a separate namespace defect. A loader check that rejects the combination would turn the silence into a hard error at regeneration time.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
