| [[id:A29102C7-8E0D-4950-93E5-231889490923][compass build fails when only busctl reaches the user manager]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | =compass= =build= =sandbox= =systemd= =tech_debt=                             | The build wraps cmake --build in systemd-run --user --scope, but systemd-run uses the plain transport, so a sandbox where only busctl reaches the user manager fails the build outright.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| [[id:156BB434-0CA9-452A-BFD8-466413BFD328][compass build scopes itself with systemd-run, which the sandbox cannot reach]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |                                                                               | Inside the DSH sandbox, compass build fails before it compiles anything: 'Failed to connect to user scope bus via local transport: No data available'. The memory-cap scope is added with a raw systemd-run --user --scope, which needs the private user-manager socket at $XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/systemd/private. That socket is unreachable here, which is exactly why systemctl_bus.py exists: it drives the same user manager over busctl on the D-Bus session bus, and that path works. So the detection and the action disagree -- _has_user_systemd() probes through the shim and answers yes, and the scope creation then fails through the raw call. Measured: 'busctl --user list' succeeds unprivileged, 'systemctl --user show-environment' fails, 'systemd-run --user --scope' fails. Two candidate fixes: route the scope through the shim, or make the check probe what it is about to do and run unscoped when it cannot. Workaround in use is an escalated shell, which is a policy ask every build should not need.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| [[id:29E7FE1A-63C1-4055-BCC5-EEBB2DA32697][compass check --only still runs the heavy phase's other members]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | =compass= =checks= =snag=                                                     | compass check --only step-consumers --include-heavy was expected to run one check, but it started the build, db and ctest closure as well and began a build while the fleet was running. --include-heavy admits the phase's own members even when --only names a single check, so --only is not literally only. Either make --only authoritative, or document that it selects within the phase rather than narrowing the phase.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| [[id:A12727BB-342C-41F1-B37D-3FB1602823FE][compass db recreate -D drops the environment's shared roles]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | =compass= =database= =defect=                                                 | Recreating a database under another name with -D still runs drop_roles.sql for the whole environment, so a scratch test database cannot be built beside a deployment that holds data; it failed safely only because the roles own objects in the deployment database.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| [[id:66B52357-6C5C-4648-922B-8D93701B0B37][Compass has no way to create a story or task without creatin…]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | =compass= =agile= =tech-debt= =paperwork=                                     | Compass has no way to create a story or task without creating a branch. 'compass story new' unconditionally runs 'git switch -c branch base' (see _scaffold_and_branch in compass.py) with no opt-out flag; 'compass task new' creates the branch object without switching (worktree stays put -- the start vs add distinction), but still creates a branch; only 'compass task start' switches. There is no branch-free scaffold path at all. Triggered while doing pure paperwork: creating an umbrella story under sprint 25 to group all trading-instrument-to-codegen port tasks, and re-parenting the existing port tasks (pilot equity_position_instrument, task 91ED2388 via PR 1857; the 5-entity batch plus remaining conversions, task F560ADDF via PR 1874; eventing facet work) into it -- 'compass task move' already supports relocating tasks between stories UUID-intact, but creating the target story forces a branch switch, which is hazardous on shared mid-flight feature branches with unmerged commits (here: feature/codegen-nats-eventing-and-integration-tests). The manual workaround is to hand-author story.org plus sprint-table row plus task orgs plus regenerate indexes, all of which the scaffold does by itself. Desired: a --no-branch flag (or a story add / task add subcommand) that scaffolds the org files and sprint row without touching git, mirroring how 'task new' already avoids the switch.                                                                 |
| [[id:A636C917-027E-4D2D-8BE0-0C9F49C7B545][compass lint runs one of the six checks in the doc-lint job]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | =ci= =documentation= =tooling= =tech_debt=                                    | The doc-lint job runs six checks and only the first is compass lint; the other five are committed build scripts the workflow invokes by path. A contributor who runs the verb named after the job gets a green result while CI can still fail, and the trigger filter names only the generate_skill scripts.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| [[id:84ABCEB9-45E5-47E5-A9C4-829564FB8B03][compass site page publishes paths the full build excludes]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | =compass= =site= =build=                                                      | compass site page hands the changed files to ores-build-page.el by name, and that path calls org-publish-file directly, so it never consults the site:pages project's :exclude regex. A page under an excluded path is therefore published by compass site page while a full build skips it. Observed: with projects/ores.dsh_kanban/test/fixtures added to the exclude regex, the page builder still published 65 fixture pages, and only stopped once the named-file loop was given its own exclusion test. The fix is to apply the project exclusion in that loop, or to route named files through the project machinery.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| [[id:90F280D4-8C96-4970-9D64-533DF3F88AF0][compass story new --kind hotfix flips the story to STARTED b…]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |                                                                               | compass story new --kind hotfix flips the story to STARTED but leaves its row in the sprint's Hotfixes table at BACKLOG, so the sprint table disagrees with story.org until the row is edited by hand. Observed on the main_ci_red hotfix: the scaffold output printed 'state: BACKLOG -> STARTED' and 'story state -> STARTED', yet the sprint row stayed BACKLOG. Either update the sprint row when the story state flips, or leave the story at BACKLOG until task start so the row is written once.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| [[id:F00A4A4D-8FFF-4F1F-9173-E954915524C7][compass task done does not flip the task's row in the story'…]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | =compass= =agile=                                                             | compass task done does not flip the task's row in the story's * Tasks table whenever the story's * Status block names that task as Next. The row updater _update_task_row_in_story (projects/ores.compass/src/compass.py:4002) walks the story line by line and acts on the FIRST line that links to the task, which is the Status table's Next row. That row splits into four cells, fewer than the six a task row has, so the function returns False and the real row, further down and correctly shaped, is never reached. The command prints no error: it simply omits its 'story * Tasks row -> DONE' line. Closing a task is exactly when the Status block tends to name it, so the failure is the common case, and the drift it leaves (task DONE, story row BACKLOG) is the silent inconsistency the runbook warns about. The candidate fix is for the loop to skip a match whose cell count is not the task table's and continue to the next match, rather than returning False on the first one, plus a unit test over a story text whose Status block names the task. Found while closing the bundle write, where the row was corrected by hand.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| [[id:4A542CE0-FF7F-4611-AE1E-ADA0737B5534][compass task done misses the story Tasks row. _update_task_r…]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |                                                                               | compass task done misses the story Tasks row. _update_task_row_in_story (projects/ores.compass/src/compass.py:4002) scans for the first line that holds the task id link and returns False when that line has fewer than six cells, so it never reaches the real Tasks row. Seen when BAE5860D closed on 2026-10-04: the story Status Next row links the task, is matched first, and the Tasks row stayed STARTED; the row was set by hand. The scan should skip lines that are not rows of the Tasks table.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |

review of ores.marketdata's generated code: lost claims report as internal errors, put_many reads back row by row, three archetypes have no generated test, generators fill foreign keys with random | [[id:B0ED2679-4BAF-4797-AB4D-AC2359BEC9C2][Delete the per-feed clock now that one scheduler drives a run]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | =synthetic= =arrival= =scheduler= =tests=                                     | Both feeds still carry a tick_clock and a clock_.run branch for the case where no scheduler was given. It is unreachable in production, since every feed_build_context sets a scheduler, and no test exercises it — so it is dead code that exists only because the feed tests and the adapter runner construct feeds without a scheduler and expect the feed to tick itself. Removing it means making the scheduler a required feed dependency and migrating those callers: the five service-test fixtures (folder cascade, config handler, selection, registry stub) and the adapter test each need their own scheduler and a thread running it, generator_runner needs the run's scheduler in the test, and the remaining tick_clock cases move to the scheduler before the clock is deleted. Attempted on 2026-10-25: everything compiled and the quant and API suites passed, but the service suite hung under ctest, so the attempt was reverted rather than shipped.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| [[id:859678B0-3755-48DF-8849-727C7250EEA5][Derive the compute node's HTTP base URL instead of copying a stale host profile]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | =compute= =deploy= =snag=                                                     | The .env.<host> profiles are gitignored hand-maintained copies with no regeneration path. goedel, newton and simba carried ORES_COMPUTE_HTTP_BASE_URL=http://192.168.1.172:20400 while the environment's HTTP server moved to 20401, so every remote job failed with 'connect: Connection refused' and the grid ran on local wrappers only for about fourteen hours before anyone noticed. The profiles are stale in other ways too: service DB ports at 5433 against 5432 in .env, and variables this environment no longer has. env deploy copies the profile faithfully, so the defect stays invisible until work is dispatched. Derive the URL from .env at deploy time, or give the profiles a generator.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

| [[id:D2374556-4672-4967-AF3F-3ADA58A9AE40][drift-sweep was red on 32 refdata generated files, and regenerating fixed it]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |                                                                               | compass check --only drift-sweep failed with 32 files that a render would change, all in ores.refdata generated output. Every diff was clang-format wrapping and include ordering, with no semantic difference, so the gate was reporting formatting drift and not a model disagreement. Regenerating the component with compass codegen regenerate --component refdata --address ores restored all 32 to the content HEAD carries: the working tree had them reformatted away from what the generator produces. The sweep is green afterwards and a second regeneration leaves the 32 clean, so the fix is stable rather than incidental. git status went from 232 to 200 modified paths. Worth knowing because the failure looks like a code change and is only a formatting one, and because the same shape could hide a real drift behind 31 cosmetic ones.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| [[id:D68B4DF9-71E0-4606-BA41-287E3B7544E4][Drive the step-consumers config assertions wrong once, with the fleet down]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | =nats= =checks= =follow-up=                                                   | The step-consumers gate asserts ack explicit, ack_wait 30s, max_deliver 10 and retention interest, which is what the step_claim_lease invariant is written against. Only the count-and-name assertion has been driven wrong: a stray consumer made it exit 1, and removing the stray returned it to 0. The four config fields have only ever been observed holding their correct values. Change each in turn on a legitimate consumer with the fleet down, record the verbatim failure for each, and restore. A stray consumer is the wrong instrument because it fails on count before the config is examined.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |

| [[id:EC7C2C75-5408-4350-AF9F-638A82EEC585][Every generated NATS handler says its reads check the permission a write needs]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |                                                                               | cpp_nats_handler.hpp.mustache carries one comment above every handler it emits: 'The adapter decides nothing: it proves the request, checks the permission a write needs, decodes the canonical request, calls the service and replies with the response the service filled.' The sentence is written once and emitted for every verb, so a read verb's comment claims a write permission and a delete verb's claims a write too. The generated handlers for ores.iam.sessions are the concrete example: list, get and get_many are now guarded with iam::sessions:read, and all seven verbs carry the same comment. The fix belongs in the template, not in one generated file, and the template is itself tangled from its org source, so the change has to be made there. Raised during the review of PR #2465, where the same rewording had been applied by hand to one generated file and was reverted by regeneration.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |

| [[id:80C846D1-4A13-4331-A88C-727D6C3D861C][Finish the eventing payload migration, or read the legacy shape]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | =eventing= =codegen= =sql= =tech_debt=                                        | postgres_event_source parses every database notification with rfl::json::read<entity_event_notification>, whose struct requires event_id, entity, key, action, version, occurred_at and tenant_id, and returns on a parse failure. 86 of the 209 *_notify_trigger_create.sql files emit that canonical shape and 123 still emit the older {entity, timestamp, entity_ids, tenant_id}. Every notification from the 123 fails to parse, so those entities publish no entity_change_event at all. Observed as 322 parse failures in the trading service log and as twenty-one failed eventing assertions in ores.trading.core.tests.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| [[id:6C0DA430-B5BC-447C-8BBC-72B4DBDE9420][Five test binaries fail on main, and four of them share one …]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |                                                                               | Five test binaries fail on main, and four of them share one cause: a generated insert function now raises 'Row already exists: a create cannot replace it' where the test expects a plain insert to succeed. Seen in ores.refdata.core.tests (ores_refdata_books_insert_fn line 91, ores_refdata_party_identifiers_insert_fn line 80, ores_refdata_portfolios_insert_fn line 111) and ores.marketdata.core.tests (ores_marketdata_market_series_insert_fn line 32, ores_marketdata_series_classification_rules_insert_fn line 34). Two eventing tests fail in the same suites because no event is published when the insert raises (counterparty_contact_information_eventing_integration_tests.cpp:178, counterparty_eventing_integration_tests.cpp:164). The fifth, ores.dq.core.tests, fails for separate reasons: ores_dq_lei_entities_tbl and ores_dq_lei_relationships_tbl are named by the code and created by no script, the change_reason_category validator rejects the code data_correction, and six generator tests assert version == 1 and get 0. Also failing: ores.dq.api.tests, on two table-rendering assertions in domain_coding_scheme_tests.cpp, and ores.shell.api.tests, on to_iso8601_utc emitting a space instead of T, which is already captured separately. Observed on 2026-09-27 with ctest --preset linux-clang-debug-make: 81 of 86 pass. None of the failing binaries belongs to a component the reporting branch touches.                                                       |

| [[id:4FC465DC-A5E0-47E5-8B05-AAC55682226E][Legacy template docs sit outside the ores.* physical-space graph]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |                                                                               | Eleven archetype-named template-library pages (doc_facet_org, doc_facet_group_org, doc_technical_space_org, doc_archetype_org, doc_profile_org, doc_feature_org, and five sql_* files) are typed knowledge, carry no facet keyword, and are named for the template they hold rather than by MASD address. Migrate each to the ores DOT facet DOT archetype filename shape with the archetype type and the facet keyword, preserving its ID and its tangle block, so the catalogue lists them.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |

| [[id:7A821ED8-764C-48FA-962A-CBA7947E449B][Make compass shell report a script abort in its exit status]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | =shell= =compass= =testing= =tech_debt=                                       | compass shell -l exits 0 even when the loaded script aborts at its first bad command, because run_host_sync returns EXIT_SUCCESS and the shell's own failure flag never reaches it. Observed during the ores.analytics clean pass: pricing_model_product_parameters put-many aborts with 'Invalid value for pricing_model_product_id: __none__' and the process still exits 0, so the 40-script V04 sweep had to be read by eye and no CI job can gate on the library.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| [[id:821A7F1C-DF26-4F0E-90B5-F70A5B62C487][Make compass site page recover from a truncated publish cache]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | =compass= =site= =tooling= =dx=                                               | Make compass site page recover from a truncated publish cache. A 0-byte build/output/org-timestamps/site:pages.cache plus a stale Emacs lock (.#site:pages.cache) left by a dead PID makes every site page run fail with 'org-publish-cache-get called, but no cache present'. The cache is derived data and rebuilds in one run, but nothing says so: the command reports a cryptic org-internals error and no recovery. Delete the stale lock and the truncated cache before publishing, or make the command detect the state and say what to do. The how_do_i_deploy_the_site recipe documents neither the failure nor the recovery.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| [[id:C548228E-1C67-4B2B-AD1E-8FFD412D81E9][Make sunday_to_monday survive regeneration of the refdata calendar rule]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | =refdata= =codegen= =snag=                                                    | Commit a1d358e5da added sunday_to_monday to projects/ores.refdata/api/include/ores.refdata.api/domain/calendar_rule.hpp, which is marked AUTO-GENERATED, and never updated the model it comes from: projects/ores.refdata/modeling/ores.refdata.calendar_rule.org line 189 still lists only none, nearest_weekday and roll_forward_to_monday, and the valid-values column at line 316 agrees with three. Any codegen run drops the fourth value from the generated header; this happened and had to be reverted by hand. The functional fix in the materialiser and its test is hand-written and survives. Fix the model, not the generated file.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| [[id:1630D3EF-FC1F-4928-A106-04BEE3238435][Make the build's systemd probe test what the build actually runs]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | =compass= =build= =sandbox= =tech_debt=                                       | compass build wraps cmake in 'systemd-run --user --scope' whenever compass_claude._has_user_systemd() passes, but the probe only runs 'systemctl --user show-environment'. In a sandbox where systemctl reaches the manager and systemd-run cannot open the scope bus, the probe passes and the build dies with 'Failed to connect to user scope bus via local transport'. The probe should exercise the thing the build depends on, or the scope wrap should honour the ORES_USE_BUSCTL transport that the rest of compass adopted.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| [[id:C90544E7-5424-4956-A02D-6BB2F65D42DC][marketdata persistence intermittently failed 2026-08-12 03:0…]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |                                                                               | marketdata persistence intermittently failed 2026-08-12 03:05:30-08:35:48 with 'Failed to persist observation' after 'No available connections in the pool': with the default pool size of 2 (database_options.hpp), one connection stuck in COMMIT (wait_event IO) from service boot at 03:01:50 for ~5.7h left one effective connection, and concurrent ingest handlers (5 ticks/s) drained it between acquires. Persistence recovered once the stuck connection resolved (~08:54); ~10,500 observations/party persisted afterward. Environmental/pre-existing fragility, not an F15 defect; consider surfacing the acquire timeout and/or raising pool size. ~479 idle Postgres connections also accumulated over 5 days (pre-existing leak, out of scope).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

P04 and M06 want operation models.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| [[id:3B2FF8B4-1607-4EFD-9638-7DC37C91DC68][Model the workflow payloads instead of storing them as documents]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | =workflow= =architecture= =data-modelling=                                    | The workflow schema holds six jsonb columns that carry serialised messages: request_json and result_json on the instance, and request_json, response_json, command_json, compensation_json and step_log_json on the step. Some of them are already typed on the wire and flattened into a document only for storage: step_log_json is a std::vector<step_log_entry>, whose shape the model already declares. Others are genuinely opaque to the engine and are relayed into the next step's builder, which parses them. Work out which of the six are models that were flattened and should be relations, which are payloads the engine must not interpret and should stay opaque, and what the engine actually needs to be able to name. The end state is that nothing in the schema holds a relation disguised as a document.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |

Instruments use the separate identity and audit group slots. | [[id:3AD9A98E-C8D0-4351-A1B7-470888A253DB][Name a set of unrelated refdata kinds that a screen or export groups]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | =architecture= =data_classification= =conventions= =bff=                      | The data classification page has no name for a set of kinds that a screen, an export or a boundary lists together with no invariant between them. The BFF convention screen is one case: it shows the document families, a composite family and two kinds the conventions document does not carry (tenor and currency pair). The page says a set that needs a screen to be true is not a bundle, and it says a list of several kinds is not a list. It also closes the set of classifications. Decide whether the page needs a new classification for such a set, or whether each case stays several lists with a small hand-written catalogue that the code states as a presentation choice. Then record the decision on the classification page. Found while the BFF family list task moved to the generated registry.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| [[id:607BFAB1-48A1-486E-84B9-CF23C603AE7A][Name the event-filled cache in the pattern language]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | =patterns= =data= =caching=                                                   | Entity-mirror caches are filled by reading the owner back on its change events, which the Cache-Aside page calls a different technique. The pattern language names no pattern for it. Find the established name, such as Event Notification with a read back, or Event-Carried State Transfer if events come to carry the rows, and write its page in the data or communication group, linking the entity-mirror cache page as its use.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| [[id:A0B50FB0-A546-4A02-B25D-D3C5C0C78238][Narrow the inbox raise to one security-definer function]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | =refdata= =trading= =sprint_28=                                               | Gated services write five inbox tables to raise a request. Give them EXECUTE on one inbox function instead, so the inbox owns the write rules and the grant surface shrinks. The call still runs in the caller's transaction, so the request and the held change still commit together. Refdata and trading both use the wide grants today (service_registry.org). Found while checking whether the trading raise crosses an owner.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |

| [[id:C3920569-BE0A-43A4-82B7-DE9739A6DB53][Nothing ends a session, so every session is an open one]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |                                                                               | ores_iam_sessions_tbl carries end_time text not null default '', and an empty end_time is what makes a session active. Nothing ever writes it: after signing out through POST /api/session and DELETE /api/session the row still has an empty end time, and a live read of the fleet's sessions found 17 rows and not one with an end time. The consequences for the credentials work are that iam.v1.sessions.active answers every session the deployment has ever created rather than the open ones, the audit screen cannot show a session that has ended or a sign-out at all, and iam.v1.sessions.end - the operation the audit and rescue screens both want, to end another account's session - has no counterpart writer behind it either. Found while implementing the active sessions read (PR #2468).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |

An operation model generates no handler, so its handler is hand-written and can ship with no permission check at all: ores.scheduler's operation handlers call has_permission zero times. The HTTP server has no route-level permission hook either, and only iam_routes checks codes.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| [[id:E9C675BD-4C5D-4D7A-A908-DE2FCC197976][On login geolocation the country lookup fails with a genuine…]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |                                                                               | On login geolocation the country lookup fails with a genuine grant or row-level-security defect, and the failure is logged and then discarded. Observed 2026-10-14 10:59:12 in the iam service journal under component ores.geo.service.geolocation_service: ERROR: permission denied for table ores_geo_ip2country_tbl. The error is raised inside PL/pgSQL function ores_geo_ip2country_lookup_fn(uuid,inet) at line 15 of its RETURN QUERY, which selects c.country_code from ores_geo_ip2country_tbl c where c.tenant_id = p_tenant_id and c.ip_range @> ip_as_bigint limit 1. The login succeeds and continues, so the caller silently loses the country; the surrounding workflow never learns that anything failed, and the workflow step that was running at the time did not use the geolocation at all, so this is an independent defect rather than the cause of that step's silence. It is either a missing grant on the table or a policy that was never applied to this database.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |

| [[id:C92DB57D-B4CC-4D50-8BC1-4AE3B630792E][ores.scheduler.api.tests fails on main since the vcpkg updat…]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |                                                                               | ores.scheduler.api.tests fails on main since the vcpkg update of 2026-10-05 (978e1e098d): cron_expression::from_string accepts the six-field '* * * * * *', which the test expects it to refuse. from_string prepends a seconds field before calling croncpp, so a six-field input reaches croncpp as seven fields, and the newer croncpp evidently accepts seven. The fix is to require exactly five fields before the prepend.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| [[id:E5553757-7D88-4B58-8057-C4A3230EE1AA][ores.shell folder feeds: start/stop folder must cover IR cur…]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |                                                                               | ores.shell folder feeds: start/stop folder must cover IR curve configs too. synthetic start folder <token> only starts fx_spot_generation_config rows (marketdata.v1.market_feed_configs.start_folder, per market_feed_config_protocol.hpp); ir_curve_generation_configs under the same folder are ignored, and ores.shell has no IR curve feed commands at all (the synthetic.service ir_curve_feed_configs.start/stop/list handlers are only reachable via raw NATS, config_id-keyed). | [[id:6DF63B9E-5A71-4D78-AC50-D387047F7537][Password recovery by emailed link does not exist end to end]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |                                                                               | Nobody can recover a forgotten password. There is no request subject and no completion subject (the candidates are iam.v1.accounts.request-password-reset and iam.v1.accounts.complete-password-reset), no table stores a recovery token or its expiry, no screen in ores.web asks for a reset, and no client has a screen that completes one. The product owner set this as the design for Rescue access while accepting its prototype: a tenant administrator sends the link, and the colleague sets a password only they know, so an administrator-typed reset becomes a collapsed fallback rather than the principal action. The journey document journey_rescue_access.org carries the corrected steps, operations table and gaps. The delivery and token machinery this needs is captured separately as the tree cannot send an email; this capture is the flow itself: who may request a reset, what the token guarantees (single use, expiry, invalidation on password change), how a completed reset interacts with the lock state and open sessions, and the two screens (member-requested and administrator-sent) that were left undrawn.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
