| [[id:BB5C313D-437B-43B8-B80C-4C811783A172][Provisioning produces duplicate temporal account rows: the s…]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |                                                                               | Provisioning produces duplicate temporal account rows: the same id and version twice, observed after a full ACME tenant provisioning run. Login still resolves the active row, so it is invisible today, but a duplicated bitemporal row is the class of defect the versioning work exists to prevent, and it stays invisible until it is not. The suspicion is a publish and version race: the DQ publish and the account write path both stating a version for the same row. Reproduce it on a recreated database by provisioning a tenant, then inspect the account tables for rows sharing id and version. Found while atomising the tenor basis swap convention, in the same run that proved stream provisioning already worked. Needs its own story: it is a correctness defect in the publish and version path, not part of any current workstream.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |

| [[id:493D1539-C092-4BD4-A6AE-709B74AE2D31][Refdata junctions bind their profile where codegen cannot read it]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |                                                                               | Four refdata junction orgs carry '#+profile: tenant-scoped-junction' in front matter. load_org_junction_model reads frontmatter through a fixed allowlist (product, schema, component, name, name_singular, name_title, name_singular_words, brief, has_tenant_id) that excludes 'profile', so the binding is silently dropped and the junction resolves profile=None, has_tenant_id=None. Rendering sql_schema_junction_create.mustache for these four emits no tenant_id column, while the committed SQL under projects/ores.sql/create/refdata/ still has 'tenant_id uuid not null' plus its RLS policy and index. The committed output is therefore stale, and regenerating refdata would silently strip tenant scoping from currency_currency_groups, party_counterparties, party_countries and party_currencies. The canonical binding point is a '* Flags' section, per _ensure_profile_binding's docstring in org_loader.py:264-276; the five junctions that use it (compute, dq x2, iam, marketdata) all resolve tenant_id=True. Fix is to move the line, then regenerate refdata and confirm the SQL is unchanged. Found while fixing the same defect in the new marketdata market_series_asset_classes junction.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |

| [[id:D3D78161-377F-4970-998F-37C7359761DA][Refuse an empty key list before delete-many renders IN ()]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | =codegen= =repository= =sql= =tech_debt= =shell=                              | A generated delete-many builds its SQL predicate with an empty IN list when the key it resolves matches no row, and Postgres rejects it with a syntax error at or near ')'. Found during the ores.analytics clean pass: the generated recipe passes the __none__ sentinel and the service answers internal_error with 'Query execution failed'. The same command answers ok when a preceding add created the row, so the condition is 'no row matched', not the entity's key type. The repository should refuse an empty key list with a validation verdict, or treat it as a no-op, rather than emit a statement that cannot parse.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| [[id:2E4FFE8F-3804-400F-BCB2-6DAA6987A498][Regenerate or retire the domain round-trip dataset (resolved)]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | =ore= =testing= =coverage=                                                    | assets/test_data/domain_roundtrip/ is the second derived artefact of external/ore/examples/ and it is badly out of step. A fresh v17 round-trip writes 566 files; the committed dataset holds 1474. Regenerating would delete 961 files: 946 are empty Portfolio stubs that an older build wrote for every XML it could not parse, and 15 are orphans for Products/SupportedTrades/, a directory upstream has since removed. It would add 53 outputs for examples the v17 sync introduced, including Products/Example_Trades/Credit_CallableBond.xml, and change 177 more, mostly conventions.xml and curveconfig.xml where the v17 schema moved. The dataset also duplicates assets/test_data/golden_dataset/, which the sprint_17 story said it had replaced; the retire task is marked DONE but golden_dataset is alive and named by 13 files. Decide which of the two is canonical before regenerating either.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| [[id:99F780E4-370A-47D9-8DE5-A7087B7AC1E3][Regenerate the stale ER diagram and gate it]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |                                                                               | projects/ores.sql/modeling/ores_schema.puml is a generated artifact (banner: GENERATED FILE - DO NOT EDIT, regenerate with ./projects/ores.codegen/plantuml_er_generate.sh) and it is a month stale. Its last commit was 6610f847fd on 2026-08-07. Regenerating it today produces 2069 changed lines and a 3 MB PNG growth, almost all of it unrelated drift: the trading bond wave, refdata catalog changes, and the marketdata classification work. No CI gate covers it, so nothing catches the drift. The market_series.is_scalar column and the market_observation.point_id nullability were deliberately left undocumented in the diagram by the point-model change set rather than dragging that month of drift into it. Work: regenerate the diagram once, wire the check into the drift gate so it cannot go stale again, and consider dropping the tracked PNG and cmapx in favour of build-only output.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |

| [[id:0EBBA137-9A83-4411-A706-DBC5543B1235][Running the workflow engine tests re-dispatches the deployment's in-flight runs]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |                                                                               | recover_in_progress() runs at engine construction and re-dispatches every in-progress step of every tenant, which is right for a deployed service resuming its runs and wrong for a test process pointed at the same database: the test re-stamps command_published_at on real runs, so their deadlines restart and a step that should have expired does not. Observed while proving the step deadline live: three test runs pushed a live run's deadline out at 11:20, 11:21 and 11:22.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

Their clean-up stories (clean-scheduler, clean-synthetic) regenerate them, which brings as_of with it; each should check its list protocols carry as_of and its services read it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| [[id:63950E15-BDE9-4074-A7CB-C02B356CB03C][scheduler instances --job filters one page, not the collection]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | =scheduler= =operations= =shell= =bug=                                        | The shell's scheduler instances command filters by job name after the page arrives, and the operation orders executions by time. A narrow --limit therefore selects the newest rows and the filter sees only those: --job e2e-ok --limit 5 printed one of that job's two executions because the other fell outside the five newest rows. The command's help says the filter is page-scoped, which is a workaround rather than a fix.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| [[id:219A14FC-B15E-459F-B654-757B898C6177][scheduler wakes on a fixed minute grid, not the next due instant]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | =scheduler= =performance= =engine= =bug=                                      | The loop arms a timer for the next wall-clock minute boundary and evaluates every job it holds on each wake, so a scheduler with one daily job wakes 1440 times a day and parses every job's cron expression every time. The due instant is known in advance: the loop should sleep until the earliest next occurrence among the jobs it holds, re-arm when the job set changes, and bound the sleep so a wall-clock adjustment cannot strand a firing.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |

| [[id:D342ECFD-EE6A-4296-BD0B-A02CC376804E][Skills are missing the frontmatter and regulatory function their contract requires]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | =llm= =skills= =claude_code= =contract= =hygiene=                             | Fifty-one of eighty SKILL.org files carry no type or level frontmatter, and none of the eighty declares its regulatory function, which the skill contract states must be declared per instance because it cannot be inferred from the type.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| [[id:9FC4C9F8-9291-4B5E-AA80-D1C7FBA038D0][Start the fleet without systemd on macOS and Windows]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |                                                                               | compass services start/stop/status are systemd-only: they generate units and drive systemctl --user, and the controller/supervisor that used to exec and cascade-stop children was decommissioned when systemd took over. macOS and Windows runners have no systemd, so the nightly jobs cannot bring NATS up: the macOS run fails with 'nats-server did not become ready within 30s' and 'no systemd user unit nats-server-orestudio.service'. The CI suites need a non-systemd supervisor path: spawn nats-server and the ores binaries with a pid file per service, keep the existing log-based readiness gate (the loop already waits on port 20005), and cascade stop through the same pid files so the stop command stays complete.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| [[id:3402E259-3F40-4F03-91FC-F6498539D89F][Stop check runs leaving generated files dirty in the working tree]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | =codegen= =checks= =snag=                                                     | compass check regenerates and compares, and on this environment the comparison leaves files modified: ores.refdata.api/domain/calendar_rule.hpp, then wire-protocol configuration_document_protocol.ts, then curve_bootstrap_config_protocol.ts, each appearing as modified within a minute of the others and sometimes showing an empty diff. Two files had to be reverted by hand in one session, and a generated header's reversion nearly went into a commit. The tree should be unchanged after a read-only check, or the check should name every file it wrote.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| [[id:15062C8E-3282-4A84-B3BC-40C286388993][Storage routes demand a token no C++ caller sends]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | =storage= =http= =shell= =defect=                                             | The storage HTTP routes call auth_required() and check a permission code per operation, but no first-party C++ caller supplies a token: ores.storage.core http_client and storage_transfer have no Authorization support at all, the shell's storage call sites pass only a base URL, and so do the services. Every shell upload and download and every service-side blob transfer therefore answers 401 -- compute publish-package, compute download-input, compute download-output, ore upload, and the ore service's report-package handler that downloads the trades and market-data blobs and uploads the tarball. The bus interface is unaffected.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

| [[id:434D8720-5764-4815-8148-693567A75AA1][The :has_batch_read: flag is inert. Twenty trading instrumen…]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |                                                                               | The :has_batch_read: flag is inert. Twenty trading instrument models set it, and template_variables.org documents it as 'Repository exposes a batch read method', but no generator source and no template reads it. The batch read read_latest(context, <primary key vectors>) is declared in cpp_domain_type_repository.hpp.mustache and implemented in cpp_domain_type_repository.cpp.mustache unconditionally, with no guard. So the flag changes nothing, and it reads as if it did. Either delete the flag from the models and from template_variables.org, or give it a consumer. Found while scoping the composite family get and put in the atomise-the-convention pilot.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| [[id:E5CBCBA4-312D-4C88-B20A-6B281EABB7F5][The @ores/bff dev script cannot start the BFF from its own package]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |                                                                               | packages/bff/package.json runs its dev script as tsx watch --env-file=../../../.env src/main.ts, and npm runs a workspace script with the working directory set to the package. Both relative paths are then wrong: the env file resolves to projects/.env, which does not exist, and site-config's default <cwd>/config/environments.json resolves to packages/bff/config/environments.json, which does not exist either. The server therefore fails to start with 'node: ../../../.env: not found' or 'Cannot read the site configuration', and it starts only when launched from the workspace root with an explicit path: ORES_WEB_PORT=21803 npx tsx watch --env-file=../../.env packages/bff/src/main.ts. Found while verifying the credentials read routes live, which needs the BFF running from source rather than from the fleet's built dist. Either the script or the site-config default should resolve from the module rather than from the caller's working directory, and the start-up note in the README should match whatever is chosen.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |

| [[id:125E8691-7538-4450-869B-FA3C73492390][The catalogue carries 253 generated files that no longer match their models]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |                                                                               | check_component_drift.py --sweep reports that no file this branch touched would change, but 253 files elsewhere in the catalogue would. The registry of components under test is a list of components that are already clean, so a component waiting its turn has no gate at all and its generated output drifts from its models unremarked. The inbox is the clearest example: 117 of its generated registrars no longer match, and the trigger was commit cf89a3edda, Bring every declared subject onto the entity protocol, which changed inbox models after 99e17bd5a8 last regenerated those registrars. Nothing regenerates on build, so the drift sits there. The sweep docstring already states the position -- a component's pre-existing drift belongs to that component's own clean-up -- so this is a capture about the size of the debt rather than a defect in the gate. Decide whether the catalogue gets a standing regeneration task, or whether each clean-up story carries its component's drift as an explicit first step.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

| [[id:06B18FA3-E010-4526-8158-6F4A8516019D][The conventions pick-lists route answers 502 or 503 on every…]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |                                                                               | The conventions pick-lists route answers 502 or 503 on every call and has done since before 2026-10-18. Two faults, both in packages/bff/src/conventions.ts. First, its PICK_LISTS loop passes only { filter: null } to readAll, but every one of those lookup subjects requires as_of, so the first entry is refused with bad_request; tenors.ts passes { filter: null, as_of: null } and works, which is the fix. Second, once as_of is passed the loop gets as far as refdata.v1.sub_periods_coupon_types.list and that subject answers 503, so the route still fails and something else is missing for it, either a handler or a subject name. Verified against the running fleet: /api/conventions/pick-lists returns 503 'Cannot reach the message bus' caused by a 503 from that subject, while /api/tenors/pick-lists returns 200. Related: the route's calendars entry also reads calendar_names rather than calendars, the same wrong catalogue the tenor pick list had; that defect is hidden behind the other two.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

| [[id:37370E1F-099D-41C1-A659-AD6ED7D4B872][The evolution screen picker and the object form name a serie…]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |                                                                               | The evolution screen picker and the object form name a series by four words and its identity fields, such as fx / EUR / fx_option / rate_lnvol · ccy=CHF. The market data URL, oresmd://fx/EUR?type=quote&instrument=fx_option..., is the one name the rest of the product uses, and the screen already opens a series from an oresmd URL in the address. Show the URL as the series name, with the words as a secondary label, so a person can copy it, paste it into a link and read the same name in the shell and the logs. The server already composes the URL from a typed identity (compose_oresmd_uri), so the screen needs no codec of its own. Open questions: whether a long URL fits a select, and whether the words stay as the visible label with the URL on copy.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |

| [[id:6F3517B3-89B6-453C-B506-22FA63DCAC81][The IAM seed-profile tests fail on a second run against the same database]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | =iam= =tests= =hygiene=                                                       | ores.iam.core.tests writes seed profiles whose code is <noun>_profile-<n>, with n counted from zero inside each process. | [[id:2CD9F675-CD64-4215-864B-1E949CD2E823][The junction RLS policy name has no identifier-length guard]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |                                                                               | The junction create archetype composes its tenant isolation policy name in mustache as {{junction.name}}_tbl_tenant_isolation_policy, because load_org_junction_model never derives an rls_table_base the way the domain entity path does in core.py. PostgreSQL truncates identifiers at 63 bytes with a NOTICE rather than an error, so a junction whose name pushes the policy name past 63 bytes silently gets a different policy name than the template intends, and two long junctions could collide on one truncated name. The longest junction today is currency_pair_convention_calendars at 62 bytes, one under the limit, and it does not opt into RLS, so nothing is broken yet. The fix is to derive rls_table_base for junctions next to sql_name_base and truncate it with the _tbl_tenant_isolation_policy suffix reserved, mirroring the domain path's longest_rls_suffix budget.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| [[id:20B2648F-FA94-4C4F-928F-BE1EC45AEB4D][The junctions of assets, compute and reporting have no history]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | =history= =junction= =codegen= =assets= =compute= =reporting=                 | Three junctions state neither a history nor change events, because their components serve no generic history: the assets image tag, the compute app version platform and the reporting report type configuration type. A link that is closed there is a row whose end is in the past, with nobody able to read it back. The components need a history dispatch and a registrar, as iam, refdata, dq, inbox and marketdata have, and then each junction can state :history: true. Composing change events in the same components is a second step, since assets, compute, inbox, marketdata and reporting compose none today.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| [[id:768B9B97-E5CB-4541-8956-3A03A5364CD7][The legacy entity event mappings remain]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | =eventing= =codegen=                                                          | One hundred and fifty-nine registrations still use the older register_mapping API and depend on the event source converting the trigger's canonical notification for them; migrate them to the generated canonical registrars and delete the older API.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| [[id:16FD540E-F5F6-4480-BA91-3342D436D3BC][The listener loss-window test races its own reconnect]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | =database= =test= =flake= =tech_debt=                                         | postgres_listener_service_reconnect_surfaces_loss_window kills the listener's backend, waits for the old backend to be gone, then sends a notification it asserts is lost forever. Nothing stops the listener reconnecting between those two steps, and when it does the notification is delivered like any other: received[0] is the lost payload and the test fails. Seen once in a full 94-suite run on 2026-10-07 (ores.database.tests, postgres_listener_service_tests.cpp:368) and again on 2026-10-16 in a gate run for a branch that touches neither the database component nor the listener; both times it passed on an immediate re-run of the suite alone and in an earlier full run of the same binary. The comment says the wait makes the loss 'guaranteed rather than racy', but expect_gone proves only that the old backend died, not that the listener has not already reissued its LISTEN. The fix is to make the window unambiguous rather than timing-dependent, for example by holding the listener from reconnecting until the lost notification has been sent, or by asserting on the connection-loss counter instead of on which payload arrived.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |

| [[id:89CD5129-06AA-448A-BED1-78F829ABC952][The session read handlers skip the permission check the catalogue grants]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |                                                                               | projects/ores.iam/core/include/ores.iam.core/messaging/session_handler.hpp guards put_session, put_many_sessions, delete_session and delete_many_sessions with iam::sessions:write and iam::sessions:delete, and list_sessions, get_session and get_many_sessions check no permission at all. The catalogue grants iam::sessions:read ('View active sessions') for exactly this purpose, and the account and login-record reads check their equivalents (iam::accounts:read, iam::login_info:read), so the three session reads are unguarded by oversight. Any authenticated caller can list the tenant's sessions, with client, address, country, byte counters and account. Found while inventorying the routes and operations the credentials journeys need; the Audit sign-ins screen would be built on these reads.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| [[id:29892436-DF05-4EB8-B2DE-C8724211ACEB][The settings build reverts a hand-added compass test]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | =codegen= =drift= =compass= =tech_debt=                                       | compass build --direct settings tangles doc/llm/claude_code_settings.org, which regenerates projects/ores.compass/tests/test_deny_bare_stash_hook.py. Commit 20cd5089d5 hand-added test_compass_does_not_run_a_denied_stash to that generated file without adding it to the org source at line 996, so every settings refresh silently deletes the test. Reproduced twice on 2026-09-23 from feature/clean-assets: run the build, the test disappears (grep count 1 to 0); git checkout restores it. Fix by adding the test block to the org source and re-tangling, or the drift returns on the next bearings next-action.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| [[id:8B61F956-013F-4693-8B3A-1D9680C4C2B3][The settings deploy reverts a generated compass test]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |                                                                               | compass build --direct settings tangles projects/ores.compass/tests/test_deny_bare_stash_hook.py from doc/llm/claude_code_settings.org, so deploying settings regenerates that file. Commit 20cd5089d5 added test_compass_does_not_run_a_denied_stash and the ast import to the tangled file and never updated the org block, so main's committed test and its source disagree. A settings deploy therefore deletes 38 lines, including the regression test that rebuilds every git stash command from compass.py and judges it with the hook's own denied — the test guarding the capture PR flow fix that same commit made. The bearings NEXT ACTION 'refresh .claude/settings.json' triggers it, and the loss reads as an unrelated working-tree edit, which also makes check_component_drift.py --all report the dirty file. Fix is to move the added test into the org source block at doc/llm/claude_code_settings.org line 996, re-deploy, and confirm the file is byte-identical.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |

| [[id:CD4A5E09-62C0-49EC-9B48-B00D3B54335B][The skills prune refuses a symlinked entry]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |                                                                               | compass build --direct skills exits 1 on this checkout. ores/prune-stale-skills (projects/ores.lisp/src/ores-build-skills.el:127) lists .claude/skills/, finds the allium entry (a symlink to .agents/skills/allium, local tooling), finds no doc/llm/skills/allium/SKILL.org source, treats it as stale publish output and calls delete-directory on it; Emacs refuses a symlink and signals 'Removing directory: Not a directory', which aborts the build before kill-emacs 0. The publish phase runs first, so the deployed skills are current; the prune never runs, so genuinely stale outputs stay. The prune should skip symlinked entries: publish output is always a real directory.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| [[id:4845D6F5-DCEF-494E-859E-F4D5A86F419A][The staff-photos branch of the Acme seeder generates SQL for…]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |                                                                               | The staff-photos branch of the Acme seeder generates SQL for column names the assets image table no longer has. projects/ores.seeder/datasets/acme_corporation/generate_sql.py writes acme_staff_photos_populate.sql with the columns key and image_id, while projects/ores.sql/create/assets/assets_images_create.sql declares id and code, so regenerating that script produces a populate that cannot run. The committed script is the correct one and disagrees with its own generator, which is why the generator's output was reverted rather than committed while regenerating the Acme portfolio scripts for the boolean flag change. The other seventeen scripts the same generator writes are in step, so the fault is in this one branch of it, and it is either a stale column list in the assets image model's reader or a rename that reached the generator and not the table.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| [[id:3A0FE17E-A9BD-408A-A401-1B62BE0ACFB4][The start_market_feeds provisioning step fails with 'NATS re…]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |                                                                               | The start_market_feeds provisioning step fails with 'NATS request timed out' on an Acme provision, although the feeds start. On swift_curie (2026-10-13, branch feature/move-the-duplicated-dq-models-onto-their-owners, which does not touch the feed path) the step on iam.v1.ops.provision_tenant_step was published at 23:58:49 and failed at 00:00:03, 74 seconds later. All five Acme parties then held 55 feed bindings each and ticks kept arriving in ores_marketdata_market_observations_tbl. The request timeout is shorter than the time the step needs to bind and start 275 feeds, so the run records a failed step for work that succeeded. Fix: answer the request before the feeds start, or raise the timeout for this step, or start the feeds per party.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| [[id:54478DD6-5A3F-47E1-9326-7611EB32428A][The surface slices journey ends with two checks: calendar ar…]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |                                                                               | The surface slices journey ends with two checks: calendar arbitrage (total variance must not fall as expiry grows at a fixed strike) and butterfly arbitrage (the density implied by each smile must not be negative). Add the two checks as server reads over a surface, and show their result under the surface plot. | [[id:6916DC9C-4CA3-4CDC-82B3-82851CBDE30B][The synthetic generator ignores a column's default_value unless the column also carries a generator block]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | =codegen= =generator-defect= =synthetic-data= =tech_debt=                     | cpp_domain_type_generator.cpp.mustache honours a column's declared default_value only when the model also supplies a generator block; with no block it assigns a random faker value, silently discarding the default. | [[id:25D92310-186E-4437-B0AD-D1A89E7F31D2][The test helpers isolate by test run, not by test. scoped_database_helper takes its tenant from the environment, so every test in one run shares that tenant, and a test binary run by hand with the .env tenant shares it across runs too. This is by design (the helper says no truncation is needed), but nothing says that a test must therefore use keys of its own. Two marketdata tests broke other suites intermittently by writing to a surface another suite reads, and a two-party import test failed on its first import because an earlier test had left the same surface in the shared tenant. Write the convention down in the helpers and in the testing guide: one currency pair or curve id per test, and fresh ids for anything a test writes. Consider a helper that makes a tenant of its own for tests that need one.]] |                                                                               | The scoped test helper shares one tenant across tests, so a surface or a series one test writes is still there for the next. The helper's comment says each test run uses an isolated tenant, but without a test tenant in the environment it falls back to the system tenant, and the plain helper does the same. Two marketdata tests broke other suites intermittently because of it, and a test that imports a file twice under two parties failed on the first import for the same reason. Make each scoped helper create and use a tenant of its own, or state in its comment that it does not, and give the marketdata tests one currency pair each only as long as that holds.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| [[id:A9B711F9-E71C-4354-85B2-C0C7A09D4C16][The timeline page formats a date with date.getMonth() where …]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |                                                                               | The timeline page formats a date with date.getMonth() where it needs getMonth() + 1, and pads neither the month nor the day, so it writes the wrong month and an unpadded day. src/timeline/Timeline.tsx:444 returns String(getFullYear()) + '-' + String(getMonth()) + '-' + String(getDate()), which for 2026-10-18 gives 2026-9-18. The correct local-date helper already exists as loadedIso in src/ui/loadedLabel.ts, which reads the local clock and pads both fields; the resolve screen uses it. Outside that page the repo has no other caller of the broken form. Fix is to reuse loadedIso and delete the local formatter.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| [[id:B3C92954-8DBC-41F3-A9ED-E1EB74313F76][The tree has no way to send an email, and password recovery needs one]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |                                                                               | The Rescue access and Entry journeys need a single-use, time-limited password-recovery link delivered to the address on the account. Nothing in the tree can send it: there is no SMTP client, no mail service and no template pipeline, and no table stores a recovery token. ores_iam_accounts_tbl carries email; ores_iam_account_credentials_tbl carries password_hash, service_password_hash and totp_secret; ores_iam_login_info_tbl carries password_reset_required and nothing token-shaped. The candidate subjects are iam.v1.ops.request_password_reset and iam.v1.ops.complete_password_reset, since neither is an entity verb. This is platform capability rather than a credentials screen, because sign-up verification, notifications and alerting will want the same delivery path, and it carries the usual security obligations: token entropy, single use, expiry, rate limiting, and not disclosing whether an address exists. Raised by the product owner while accepting the Rescue access prototype, after the screen was changed from an administrator-typed password to a recovery link.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |

| [[id:9382EC38-7BB8-456A-AA23-D26C62B9C231][The web's generic list registries still decide per resource …]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |                                                                               | The web's generic list registries still decide per resource whether a list sends as_of. records.ts and classifications.ts carry an asOf flag and add as_of only where it is set. Since PR 2683 every generated entity list accepts as_of, so the flag describes nothing: a resource without it cannot ask for a past instant, though the server would answer. Retire the flag, or set it everywhere, and let every list offer the point-in-time read.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
